# Sorted backlog (written 2026-09-06)

Working rules for any session picking an item: branch `docs/mobile-porting-checkpoint`; UAT is this VPS (`systemctl restart wealth-api` / `wealth-worker` / `wealth-frontend` after `npm run build`); production is Vercel + Railway and is NOT touched by restarts here. Never commit `backend/.env` or any key file. Fable plans, Sonnet agents edit and commit, commit trailers `Co-Authored-By: Claude <model> <noreply@anthropic.com>`. No em dashes in user-facing copy. Design changes go through Kevin as coded variants on `/design/*` before touching production components. Tick items here as they land; add the commit hash.

Context documents: `docs/compliance/finexer-agent-controls-2026-09.md` (due diligence answers, `[KEVIN]` markers), `docs/pricing/tiering-unit-economics-mcp-2026-09.md` (tier table, unit economics, scaling, MCP design), `PENNY_TOOLS.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `PRIVACY.md`, `TERMS.md`, `DEPLOY.md`.

Board: the private page /ops/go-live on UAT reads and edits these files. Sessions use `scripts/backlog.py` (`start`, `block`, `done <id> --commit <sha>`, `note`) so the file, the page and git history stay one thing. Never tick by hand.

  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Split 2026-09-14: the BOT_SECRET replacement moved to A28 and edge/transport hardening (security headers on both hosts, catch-all rate limiting, Vercel Firewall and Cloudflare decisions) is tracked as A27, since neither was a sensible unit of work inside this bundle. A26 keeps the scope document, the OAuth threat model, webhook replay and forgery tests, the secrets-in-logs audit, the design-route data check, secret rotation docs, CI dependency and container scanning, the public disclosure route and the remediation SLA.
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-15: reviewed AFTER merge, because this landed in the same integrate pass as A28 before I had looked at it; integrate takes everything in review at once and I only checked the board immediately before the pass. Verdict on the production code is that it is correct and nothing needs reverting. The only functional change is in backend/app/routers/oauth.py, closing an authorisation-code and refresh-token double-redemption race; no existing check was removed, relaxed or reordered, and the only request that now fails is the second of two racing to redeem the same one-time credential, which was never a legitimate flow. The OAuth 2.1 fundamentals were already sound and are untouched: PKCE S256 only with plain refused, exact redirect_uri matching rather than prefix, constant-time verifier comparison, codes and tokens stored hashed, no logging statement anywhere in the file, and no redirect on an unknown client. scripts/session.sh gains a design-data check that runs on finish and can block it, writes nothing and costs under a second. The new frontend check fails only on a coarse whole-file regex list, so a legitimate build could trip on the word fetch inside a comment or string, worth knowing; its failure output prints only the path and a static reason, never matched content, so it cannot leak. The pentest scope document discloses ciphertext lengths and env var NAMES but no values, which is what a tester needs, and contains no credential, internal IP or host path. One follow-up raised separately: the two flagship concurrency tests cannot fail against the bug they guard.
- [x] **A27. Edge and transport hardening, none of which is covered by A26 (found 2026-09-14 by probing both live hosts): the production website sends only Strict-Transport-Security and without includeSubDomains or preload, so it has no Content-Security-Policy, no frame protection, no X-Content-Type-Options, no Referrer-Policy and no Permissions-Policy, and frontend/next.config.ts sets no headers block at all; the API on Railway sends no security headers whatsoever while serving bank data, because backend/app/main.py adds only CORS and GZip middleware; and core/ratelimit.py RULES covers just seven auth, webhook and push prefixes with no catch-all, so every data endpoint (transactions search, safe-to-spend, accounts, spend verdict, cashflow) is unlimited and is protected today only by ALLOWED_EMAILS keeping sign-up closed to ten addresses, which stops being true the moment D1 opens sign-up; add a headers block to the Next config and a security-headers middleware to FastAPI, add a sensible catch-all rate limit keyed by authenticated user rather than IP with tighter limits on expensive endpoints, and make the Redis-unavailable fallback safe on multiple replicas ahead of E5; Kevin separately owns configuring Vercel Firewall rules and deciding whether Cloudflare goes in front of the API, so record those as his decisions in a note rather than doing them** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, 158689b11d871f6802daf679c7b198b6a1f64ff2)

What checked out: every header is live on both hosts and correct; the catch-all degrades to a clean 429 with Retry-After and never a 500; auth, webhooks, logo and mcp paths are correctly exempted before the catch-all runs, so login, OAuth and the Stripe webhook receiver cannot be starved, which was the failure mode most worth avoiding; the Redis sliding window has a genuine in-process fallback that never raises into the request path, and with one uvicorn worker today the fallback is equally strong; the Capacitor build genuinely escapes headers() via MOBILE_EXPORT; and the reasoning for allowing unsafe-inline is accurate, since Next injects its own per-build inline flight scripts, with no analytics or error-reporting scripts present to hash instead. The new rate-limit tests are real, proved by checking out the parent commit where two files fail to import at all and four of five middleware tests fail red. Three things came out of it, each raised as its own item: the CSP blocks the Google favicon fallback so nine major UK banks silently lose their logos, which also means we were leaking bank names to Google until now; client_ip trusts X-Forwarded-For in a way that is safe behind UAT nginx but unverified through Vercel and Railway; and six expensive endpoints share one thirty-per-minute budget that ordinary browsing may exhaust. One gap left unraised: security_headers.py has no test of its own, so a future middleware-ordering change in main.py could drop every header with nothing in CI to notice.

No lockout on deploy either: every bot-eligible admin and subscription route keeps an owner break-glass path, so if UAT restarts with zero credentials minted Kevin loses nothing and can still drive every admin surface from his own login, while an external caller presenting the old secret now gets a clean 401 rather than a silent pass. A sweep of crontab, systemd units and the repo found no other consumer this merge would strand. The rest verified: secrets.token_urlsafe(32) for 256 bits from a secure source, stored only as a hash and never in plaintext, no equality comparison on a secret anywhere since verification is a hashed lookup, scopes cross-checked route by route against every actual decorator with an exact match, the user-data routes refused even to a credential holding every scope, the resolved bot principal carrying email None so it can never resolve to Kevin identity or read his financial data which was the original harm, revocation immediate with no cache anywhere in the file, and the audit row proved not to contain the token. The 406 lines of tests are real: swapping in main versions of the nine changed files makes all 45 fail, and the full suite passes at 2397. One doc inconsistency was found and fixed on the branch as b891ae6 before merge: RELEASE.md copy-pasteable operator prompt still said to generate BOT_SECRET, six lines under prose this same branch had corrected, which mattered because that block is written to be pasted verbatim, so someone would have generated a value nothing reads and believed the bot was provisioned.

- [x] **A30. Yapily consent tokens are stored in plaintext while TrueLayer tokens are Fernet-encrypted under TOKEN_KEY (found by the A26 readiness pass, 2026-09-14, verified read-only without printing any value: TrueLayer rows carry the gAAAAA prefix, Yapily collections hold 0 live rows today but the code path is live and mounted): a Yapily consent token is functionally a bank credential and must be encrypted at rest on the same terms; note the Finexer migration intends to remove dormant Yapily entirely, so decide whether to encrypt it or remove the path, but do not leave a live code path that would write a bank credential in the clear** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-14, 3b033725455a3f06f03a9472528bbe00f2e3d692)

The startup addition at main.py:534 is a fire-and-forget asyncio.create_task that is never awaited, so nothing inside it can propagate and it cannot stop the API booting; there is no assertion on the token key anywhere, and crypto.py already tolerates a missing key by generating and persisting one on first run, so the current healthy boot is by design rather than luck of configuration. Backward compatibility is handled properly: decrypt_token returns any value without the Fernet prefix unchanged, so a plaintext row written seconds before deploy reads fine, while a value with the right prefix under a wrong or rotated key returns None and _resolve_consent_token then fails closed and no-ops rather than syncing with garbage, which is the correct direction for a bank credential. All three write paths encrypt, routers/yapily.py:78, yapily_sync.py:43 and the defensive re-encrypt at yapily_sync.py:80, and a grep found no fourth writer. Nothing logs or returns the token: only counts are logged, and the endpoint that used to hand back the raw consent token now returns a fingerprint instead, which is a net improvement. Checked against A31, merged alongside, and the two do not interact badly, since yapily.py has no handler that interpolates exception text.

Verdict is follow-up needed on the test only, nothing live is wrong. The fix is complete and consistent: every one of the roughly thirty sites sampled across penny_tools.py, can_i.py, transactions.py and pdf.py now logs the real error with exc_info and returns a generic message, so the operability trade was made correctly rather than swallowing the detail. The new test IS a real regression test, proved by swapping the four source files for their pre-merge versions, where it fails and names all forty-one historical leak sites. Its limit is that it only catches zero-hop leaks: storing the exception in a variable and returning that variable passes the scan, demonstrated directly. - [x] **A32. Bot credentials never expire and an unknown token leaves no audit row** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, b4a38002c31bcb1b32e4e972cd1b87e7d867dfc5)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by the A28 security review 2026-09-15, flagged rather than fixed because neither is in that item literal text and A28 is the far larger win. Two gaps remain in the new bot credential system. First, there is no expires_at field at all, only manual revocation, so a minted credential lives forever unless a human remembers to revoke it; that is the same class of problem A28 was opened to fix, a long-lived credential nobody is forced to rotate, just much better scoped now. Decide whether credentials should carry an expiry by default, and if so whether expiry is enforced on every request or surfaced as a warning, since a bot whose credential silently expires mid-job is its own failure mode. Second, the audit trail only records USES OF RESOLVED CREDENTIALS: an unknown or forged token is refused but leaves no row at all, so there is nothing to detect probing against. Low urgency given 256-bit tokens make brute force infeasible, but an audit trail that cannot show you an attempt is a weaker instrument than one that can, and a pentester will say so, which is the audience A28 was written for. Consider recording refused attempts with the token hashed or truncated, never raw.
- [x] **A33. The two OAuth concurrency tests A26 added cannot fail against the vulnerability they were written to guard** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 2e81fe6e89b8005fb3e780f29f6382af841e7d3b)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A26 (a663eca) on 2026-09-15. THE PRODUCTION FIX IS SOUND and needs no action: backend/app/routers/oauth.py:391-420 and :443-461 replace a find_one then update_one pair with a single conditional find_one_and_update, closing a genuine TOCTOU race where an attacker racing an intercepted authorisation code against the legitimate client could previously mint two live token pairs from one single-use credential. Mongo serialises writes to a document, so exactly one caller now wins and the loser falls through to the existing revoke-the-whole-family path, which is the right behaviour since a lost race is indistinguishable from replay. THE TESTS ARE THE PROBLEM. test_concurrent_code_exchange_only_one_winner and test_concurrent_refresh_rotation_only_one_winner in backend/tests/test_oauth_server.py both PASS against the pre-fix code, proved by swapping oauth.py for its parent commit 1d061ac and running them: 2 passed, 0 failed. The reason is that they force the interleaving with a rendezvous gate patched around find_one_and_update on the fake collection, and the pre-fix code calls update_one, so the gate never fires, the two coroutines never actually interleave, and the assertions pass for the wrong reason via the pre-existing sequential-reuse path that an older test already covers. The guard is coupled to the very call the fix introduces, so it can only ever pass. Fix by gating a seam that exists in BOTH implementations, for example triggering on the first write call whatever its name, or gating update_one as well, then PROVE it by running against 1d061ac and showing it go red. This is the fourth time on this board a test has passed for the wrong reason, after B33, B34 and B36, and the first where the test was guarding a security fix rather than a behaviour, which is why it is worth doing properly rather than deleting. Separately and not part of this item: the 234 new lines in test_truelayer_webhook.py are coverage of unchanged code rather than regression tests, which is fine but should not be cited as evidence A26 fixed anything.
- [x] **A34. Nine major UK banks lost their logos when A27's CSP landed, because the curated-bank fallback fetches favicons from Google** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, 3bd7063ad256f5c4dd3e6c5f9b60eef6bd336e3b)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A27 (158689b) on 2026-09-15, live on UAT now and due to reach production at the next release. A27 set img-src to self, data: and blob:, with no allowance for www.google.com. The curated bank-icon resolver falls back to the Google favicon service for any bank without a bundled local file: googleFaviconURL in components/AccountMiniCard.tsx:193-194 and 234, duplicated in HomeBrief.tsx:125, PaydayPlanCard.tsx:21, TransactionSheet.tsx:188, ReconnectStrip.tsx:58, CardTermsSheet.tsx:52 and app/planning/dismissed/bankBadge.ts:24. Of the twelve curated BANK_META entries with a domain, only five also carry a logoFile, so HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct and TSB all resolve to a blocked URL. It degrades to the initials chip rather than a broken image, so nobody will report it as broken, they will just quietly stop seeing bank logos across Home, Accounts, the Payday Plan, transaction detail, reconnect prompts and card terms. NOTE THE PRIVACY ANGLE BEFORE CHOOSING A FIX, because it inverts the obvious one: until A27, every user browser was telling Google which banks that person holds, on every page that rendered an account. The CSP has stopped that. So re-allowing www.google.com in img-src would restore the logos by restoring a data leak to a third party, which sits badly with a product whose whole pitch is being trusted with someone finances. The better fixes are to route these through the app own same-origin /logo/ proxy, which already exists and is what the other logo path uses, or to bundle local files for the nine affected banks. Recommend the proxy, since it fixes every future bank too rather than nine. Also worth folding in: that googleFaviconURL helper is duplicated across seven files, the same copy-in-two-places pattern that keeps costing this board, so whatever the fix, it should end up in one place.
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: reviewed AFTER merge, since this went through an integrate pass I did not run. The important thing is right: the CSP was NOT widened, confirmed live as img-src self, data: and blob: unchanged, and the browser-side Google favicon call is gone from the code entirely rather than being allowed back in. The privacy reasoning is written into the code comment where the next person will find it. Consolidation was done properly too, eleven files down to one bankLogoSrc with ninety lines deleted against sixty-one added, rather than eleven hand-matched copies, which is the pattern that has cost this board repeatedly. The one thing to know is that this closes the leak without restoring the logos: the nine banks without a bundled asset now render the initials chip permanently. That is a defensible choice and the comment argues it, but it is not the only one available, because the same-origin /logo/ proxy at backend/app/routers/logos.py:94 already fetches these server-side and was verified returning real PNGs for HSBC, Monzo and Revolut. Raised separately rather than as a defect here.
- [ ] **A35. A27's IP rate limit trusts X-Forwarded-For, which is safe on UAT's nginx but unverified through Vercel and Railway** [owner: kevin] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by the A27 post-merge review 2026-09-15. client_ip() in backend/app/core/ratelimit.py:93-108 reads X-Real-IP first, then the first entry of X-Forwarded-For, then the socket peer, with no trusted-hop count. On UAT that is safe and was verified live: the nginx site config sets X-Real-IP to remote_addr unconditionally, and nginx overwrites rather than appends, so a client-forged header cannot win. Production is a different topology and could not be verified from here without hitting it: Vercel proxies at its edge to Railway, and Next own proxy path sets x-forwarded-for only if absent, meaning it preserves a client-supplied value rather than overwriting it. If either hop passes attacker input through, the anonymous-caller IP limit that A27 exists to add is trivially bypassed in production while looking solid on UAT. Impact is bounded, an abuse throttle bypassed rather than any data exposure, which is why this is p2 rather than p1. Kevin item because answering it means checking Vercel and Railway behaviour or running a controlled test against production, neither of which a session should do unprompted. If it turns out a hop is untrusted, the fix is to count trusted hops from the right rather than taking the leftmost entry.
- [x] **A36. Six expensive endpoints share one 30-per-minute budget, which ordinary browsing may exhaust** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, a08250ca9ac3375bb3ef5638e5ab9c6089d4beee)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by the A27 post-merge review 2026-09-15. EXPENSIVE_PREFIXES in backend/app/core/ratelimit.py:86-89 covers /transactions/search, /safe-to-spend, /cashflow, /spend/verdict, /money-shape and /savings-insights, and all six share ONE bucket of 30 requests per 60 seconds keyed on the user, not one bucket each. Those six cover nearly every substantive screen: Home Safe-to-Spend, the Spend verdict, the money shape, Upcoming and Planning cashflow, savings-insight callouts and transaction search. One call site actively works against the budget: fetchVerdictData in frontend/lib/verdictCache.ts:24-32 fires a real network call on every invocation unless one is already in flight, deliberately, so a stale verdict never survives a paint, and it is called independently from HomePage.tsx:404 on every Home mount, SpendTrends.tsx:1425 and SpendPage.tsx:360. So browsing Home to Spend to Shape to Upcoming a few times in a minute, or opening a couple of tips, or running one search, can consume a large share of thirty. The suite does not catch this: test_auth_middleware_catch_all.py:110-127 models four Home reloads at three expensive hits each, twelve in total, and never models moving between screens. Nobody has hit it yet as far as anyone knows, and the failure is a clean 429 with Retry-After rather than an error, but a legitimate user being throttled for using the app is worse than the abuse it prevents. Fix by splitting the budget per prefix, raising it, or making the verdict cache check itself before firing; and extend the realistic-load test to walk between screens rather than reloading one.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP. The fix is the per-prefix split rather than a raised limit: the key changed from one shared catchall-user-expensive bucket to one per prefix per user, with the numbers unchanged at 30 per 60 seconds. The gap this item was actually about is closed: new tests walk Home to Spend to Upcoming to Planning and back using request paths read off the real frontend call graph rather than guessed, and the old code genuinely throttles that walk on lap three at /cashflow, which is the bug. Four new tests fail red against main. ACCEPTED TRADE-OFF WORTH NAMING: a caller rotating across all six expensive prefixes can now draw up to 180 expensive requests a minute from one identity before any 429, against 30 under the shared bucket. Single-endpoint hammering is bounded exactly as before at 30, and the unchanged per-user 300 and per-IP 600 catch-alls still bound the total, but the ceiling for spread-out abuse is six times higher. That is the intended shape of the fix rather than an oversight, and it is the price of not throttling ordinary browsing.
- [ ] **A37. A31's exception-leak guard misses any handler that stores the exception in a variable first** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of A31 (0ad6138) on 2026-09-15. The fix itself is complete and needs no action: all roughly thirty sites across penny_tools.py, can_i.py, transactions.py and pdf.py now log with exc_info and return a generic calm message, and nothing was found still interpolating a caught exception into a user-visible string. The guard is the gap. backend/tests/test_no_raw_exception_leak.py walks every except-as block via AST and flags a return or raise whose subtree contains the bound exception name. The reviewer proved it genuinely fails red against the pre-fix code, correctly listing all forty-one old leaky sites, so it is a real regression test and not decoration. But it was also shown to MISS one hop of indirection: a handler written as except Exception as e, then msg = str(e), then return an error containing msg, passes the scan, while the same-statement version is caught. The scan inspects only the AST of the return expression, never the dataflow of the bound name through earlier statements. That is the same weakness as G62, a test carried by matching source shape rather than exercising behaviour, just implemented with ast rather than regex, and the commit message calling it AST-based oversells it. Storing an exception in a variable before returning is an ordinary thing to write, so this will eventually let one through. Either extend the scan to follow an assignment whose right-hand side embeds the bound name, and then check downstream returns for that assigned name, or change the docstring to state plainly that only zero-hop leaks are caught, so nobody trusts it further than it deserves. Prefer the first.
- [ ] **A38. Bundle real bank logos from a licensed source, replacing the brand-coloured initials chips A34 left in place (2026-09-15): A34 removed the Google favicon fallback because it disclosed which banks a user holds to a third party on every page load, and fell back to BankBadge initials for the eleven affected institutions (HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct, TSB, plus MONO and MPESA), four of which Kevin himself holds; the A34 agent tried scraping each bank's own apple-touch-icon and got 404s, an HTML redirect page mislabelled as a PNG, and inconsistent sizes, so it deliberately shipped no scraped assets; the backend already has Logo.dev wired with a LOGODEV_TOKEN slot, so use that or each bank's official brand kit; note the licensing position it established: nominative fair use covers the trademark question under TMA 1994 s.11(2) and is standard fintech practice, but copyright is separate, simple wordmarks are likely below the originality threshold while graphic marks like the HSBC hexagon and Nationwide arrow are not, and Wikipedia fair-use tags do not transfer to commercial use; the five already-bundled logos carry no provenance documentation, so add a NOTICE file recording the source and basis for every logo shipped** [owner: claude]
- [ ] **A39. The nine missing bank logos can be restored through the same-origin proxy we already have, with no privacy cost** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by a post-merge review of A34 (3bd7063) on 2026-09-16. A34 IS CORRECT AND SHOULD NOT BE REVERTED: it left the CSP alone, removed the browser-side Google favicon call entirely, and consolidated a helper that had been duplicated across eleven files into one bankLogoSrc, which is exactly the right shape and closed a real leak, since that request told Google from the user own browser and IP which banks that person holds, on every page that rendered an account. What it did NOT do is restore the logos. bankLogoSrc now returns a local file or null, so the nine curated banks without a bundled asset render the initials chip permanently: HSBC, Monzo, Revolut, Santander, Halifax, Nationwide, Chase, first direct and TSB. Only five bundled files exist in frontend/public/banks, amex, barclays, lloyds, natwest and starling. THE LOGOS ARE RECOVERABLE WITH NO PRIVACY COST and the route already exists. backend/app/routers/logos.py:94 serves GET /logo/{domain}, trying Logo.dev first and falling back to Google favicons SERVER-SIDE, and frontend/lib/api.ts already exports logoUrl for it. Because the server makes that request, Google sees the server address rather than the user, cannot tie it to an individual, and the response is same-origin so the CSP is satisfied unchanged. Verified against the live UAT API on 2026-09-16: /logo/hsbc.co.uk returned 200 with a 21894 byte PNG, /logo/monzo.com 200 with 9953 bytes, /logo/revolut.com 200 with 6857 bytes. So the fix is to have bankLogoSrc fall back to logoUrl(meta.domain) when there is no bundled file, which restores all nine and every future curated bank, rather than bundling nine more assets by hand. Check the proxy caching and its 404 path first, since a bank the upstreams miss should still land on the initials chip rather than a broken image, and confirm the existing onError fallback in BankBadge still covers that. Kevin call if he would rather keep initials as a deliberate look, but he should know the choice is available and costs nothing in privacy.

## B. Penny cost, tiers and billing

- [x] **B1. Shorten the grow cache.** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] `GET /grow` caches up to 6 h, `GET /safe-to-spend` 90 s, so Planning and Home can show different figures for the same gap (£1,053.91 vs £749 seen 2026-09-06). Entitlements read the effective tier: allowance rises immediately on upgrade with the used count carried over; on a downgrade that takes effect with more connected accounts than the new cap, do not disconnect anything, freeze the extras read-only and ask which to keep. Cancel keeps access to period end.

- [x] **B36. Two entitlement bugs in _handle_subscription_upsert found by B25's live Stripe matrix on 2026-09-13: status_map has no 'incomplete' (nor 'paused') and defaults unknown statuses to 'active', so a subscriber who abandons the 3D Secure challenge and never pays is granted the paid tier; and current_period_end moved to the subscription item in Stripe API version 2026-08-26.dahlia so expires_at is never written, meaning a past_due subscriber's dunning grace never ends and a cancel-at-period-end never takes effect; make the status mapping explicit and fail closed, read the period end from the item with a subscription-level fallback, and consider pinning the API version so a field cannot silently move again** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-13, d8a68a6af0918024b9c10c57c733a19f6557a69f)
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Fixed the test defect flagged in review: test_past_due_subscriber_keeps_tier_until_expires_at_then_loses_it manually overwrote expires_at without ever checking the value the code computed, so it passed against pre-fix billing.py too. Chose the assert-before-mutating approach (kept alongside its sibling tests style) over monkeypatching datetime.now: added an assertion right after handle_event that fake_subs.docs[0]["expires_at"] equals the items[0].current_period_end timestamp, before the existing manual overwrite that simulates time passing. Verified in /tmp scratch worktree (removed after): RED against mains pre-fix billing.py -> AssertionError: assert None is not None at the new assert stored_expires_at is not None line; GREEN against the branchs own billing.py -> 1 passed. Docstring corrected to describe what the test verifies. Full backend suite from the worktree: 2350 passed. Committed 9d2d425 and pushed to feature-B36-billing-entitlement-bugs. Item left in review, no state change.
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-14: independently reviewed and returned SHIP on the production code, with one test defect found and since fixed on the branch as 9d2d425. What the reviewer confirmed: every one of Stripe eight documented subscription statuses is mapped explicitly and the default changed from active to expired, so an unrecognised future status now withholds entitlement instead of granting it, which was the actual bug. Equally important, it does not cut off anyone who has paid: past_due and unpaid are unchanged and still hold the paid tier until expires_at passes, so an ordinary card retry does not strip access, and the two statuses newly moved to expired, incomplete and paused, both describe subscriptions that never had a successful payment. Three of the five new tests were proved genuine by running them against the pre-fix code, where they fail red. The defect was the fourth: test_past_due_subscriber_keeps_tier_until_expires_at_then_loses_it overwrote the expires_at value it was meant to be testing, so it passed against the broken code too, the same shape as B33 and B34. Now fixed by asserting the stored expires_at matches the item timestamp before the overwrite, verified red against pre-fix code with "assert None is not None" and green on the branch, full suite 2350 passed. No conflict with B33 to_dict or B34 conftest helper, and the pinned STRIPE_API_VERSION is in scope per the item text.
- [x] **B37. Penny loop retries transient provider errors and stops reporting an infrastructure failure as out of scope** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-14, f99397c28940fc0d40a31108d3b1919c37c8b3ff)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in a 2026-09-14 audit of Penny's harness against LangChain's custom-agent-harness checklist. app/services/penny_agent.py returns None on any OpenRouter non-200 (no retry at all), and app/routers/can_i.py line 349 turns that None into the deterministic refusal with out_of_scope true, so a 429 or a 502 tells the user their answerable question was outside what Penny can work out. The comment at can_i.py line 365 records this firing twice on genuinely answerable questions on 2026-08-31, where the fix applied was to reword the refusal rather than separate the causes. Two changes wanted: one bounded retry on 429 and 5xx inside the existing wall-clock budget, and a distinct failure reply with out_of_scope false so infrastructure never speaks as scope.

  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in the same 2026-09-14 harness audit. The loop logs tools used and rounds at INFO and meters cost per round to llm_usage_col, but nothing persists which tools were chosen for which question shape and nothing can replay a question. The routing bug that caused the whole loop-first rebuild (PENNY_TOOLS.md) has no regression net. Cheapest useful version: record the tool sequence on the existing usage doc, plus a golden set of roughly 30 questions drawn from docs/penny/question-inventory run against a fake model in CI, asserting tool selection rather than phrasing.
- [x] **B39. Penny knows which screen you are on but not what it is showing, so a question about the page in front of you gets answered from raw tools instead of the view (Kevin 2026-09-14): PennyAskContext in components/PennySheetProvider.tsx carries only a route name from a closed set of ten plus an optional summary string that its own comment calls decorative context, not required; exactly one surface in the whole app supplies that summary (app/spend/shape/ShapePage.tsx line 78) and PennyConversation.tsx gates it with summaryConsumedRef to the FIRST request per screen, so even on that one page a follow-up question arrives with no grounding at all; give every screen a structured, deterministic context object describing what is actually rendered, the route, the active period or filter or scope, the headline figures with the labels the user can see, and the ids of the visible cards, computed by the same code that renders them so the two can never disagree; carry it for the whole conversation from that screen rather than one shot, refresh it when the view changes, and pass it to the model as facts it may phrase but never derive from, per the engine doctrine; it must respect the no-app-artifacts rule so Penny explains or adds rather than reading the screen back, and it must carry no account numbers, sort codes or provider ids since it enters the prompt; add per-screen tests asserting the context matches the rendered figures, and start with the screens where the mismatch bites most, Home's Safe-to-Spend, Spend's period view and Upcoming** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 5f4635b550ded1fb400cce9cb7060ab12131e4ec)
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP, with the two questions that mattered most answered properly. WHAT LEAVES THE DEVICE is a fixed allow-list, not a data dump: route, scope, verdict, an as-of stamp and at most six figures whose values are PRE-FORMATTED STRINGS such as "£83" rather than raw numbers, capped on both client and server, so the shape itself has no field a transaction list or an account number could occupy. The real trust boundary is server-side in can_i.py, which re-sanitises and additionally drops any figure whose label or value pattern-matches a sort code, an eight-digit account number or an IBAN, dropping the whole figure rather than redacting it, with tests proving a legitimate neighbouring figure survives. Cost is bounded by construction at roughly 200 to 300 tokens worst case and 60 to 90 on a realistic screen, and it cannot grow with a list because there is no list field. BALANCE HIDING IS RESPECTED CORRECTLY: when balances are hidden the figures are OMITTED entirely rather than masked and sent, while the verdict word is still published, which matches the screen, since the status chip is never blurred. Flow figures on Spend and Upcoming are not gated by hide-balances on screen either, consistent with DESIGN.md separating flows from positions, so sending them agrees with the screen rather than contradicting it. SafeToSpendCard keeps all five render paths including error, degraded and unsupported, never returns null, and its hero arithmetic is unchanged. No new tool, no write access, and the server-decided verdict rule was extended rather than weakened. Raised separately: its new test is genuine, proved by mutation, but is wired into no gate.
- [ ] **B40. canPurchaseInApp resolves to web during server-side rendering because there is no Capacitor bridge in the Node build (noted by the B31 review, 2026-09-14, not fixed there): it is safe today only because every purchase surface happens to wait on an async fetch before rendering the control, which is an accident of the current code rather than a guarantee; make the native check resolve after mount, or carry an explicit unknown state that no purchase surface treats as web, so a future surface that renders synchronously cannot show a Stripe purchase path inside the native app** [owner: claude]
- [ ] **B41. Tax is the one screen Penny can reach but cannot see, because no page ever publishes its view context** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Found by the G86 review 2026-09-16, a pre-existing gap rather than anything G86 caused, and explicitly NOT a regression: setPennyScreenView has never been called for Tax, before or after that change. B39 added per-screen VIEW context so Penny knows not just which screen you are on but what it is showing, and wired it into Spend, Planning and the Safe-to-Spend card. Tax was not included. So a question asked from /tax now opens Penny with the right screen key and the right four chips, since G86 added the case "/tax" line to screenForPathname, but with no description of what the page is actually showing. The practical effect is that asking about the figure in front of you on Tax gets answered from raw tools rather than from the view, which is precisely the problem B39 was opened to fix. Tax is a good candidate for it: the page states a verdict, the 60 percent trap or a fully tapered allowance, and carries a small number of pre-formatted figures, which is exactly the bounded shape the VIEW contract expects, at most six figures as strings. Follow B39 pattern rather than inventing a second one, and respect the same rule it established, that when balances are hidden the figures are omitted rather than masked. Worth doing after the Canvas Before Cards programme settles, since the Tax page is being rebuilt underneath it.

- [x] **F18. Harden MCP tool descriptions so an external harness inherits Penny's quote-verbatim contract** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 1c40ab09ff223340bb022b7823eae27d9363906f)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found in the same 2026-09-14 harness audit. app/routers/mcp.py hands the same TOOL_SCHEMAS to an external harness such as Claude.ai, which has none of penny_agent.py's _SYSTEM_PROMPT. The rule that pre-formatted GBP must be quoted verbatim and that the model never does arithmetic lives only in that prompt, so through the connector an external harness can sum two of our figures and present the result as ours. Masking (mcp_mask) and scopes are solid, the semantic contract is not. Fix is per-tool description hardening, stating inside each tool's own description what must be quoted and what must not be recombined.

- [x] **G6. Safe-to-Spend cards-short state should show the net shortfall figure in the hero, not £0, with severity driven by the net position** [owner: claude] (done 2026-09-08)
  - note (2026-09-06, claude): Context (2026-09-06, Kevin + Fable): short_reason_for in backend/app/services/net_position.py splits short into bills (cash after set-asides <= 0, red, hero = full gap) and cards (cash positive, card growth pushes net below zero, amber, hero forced to £0 in frontend/components/SafeToSpendCard.tsx). Result: a £10 cash gap plus £100 on cards reads as £110 red, while bills covered plus £1,000 on cards reads as £0 amber, so the worse position looks calmer. Kevin's decision: show the net figure for the shortfall in the cards case too. Still to discuss when picked up: caption wording, whether chip severity escalates on the net figure or a threshold, and keeping bills-at-risk as its own red signal. Design change: propose to Kevin before building.
  - note (2026-09-07, kevin): Re-raised by Kevin 2026-09-07 with the worked example: bills covered with £71 spare plus £846 of unpaid card growth shows a £0 amber hero, whereas £10 short on bills plus £50 on cards would show £60 short in red. Bills covered plus £1,000 on cards should not read calmer than the smaller cash gap. Backend: card_growth_unpaid reserve in routers/analytics.py step 6c and short_reason_for in services/net_position.py; frontend clamp in components/SafeToSpendCard.tsx. Still to agree before build: caption wording, whether chip severity escalates on the net figure or a threshold, and keeping bills-at-risk as its own red signal.
  - note (2026-09-08, kevin): Decision reversed by Kevin 2026-09-08 after the research pass: keep the £0 hero in the cards-short state (the Simple Safe-to-Spend precedent) rather than introduce a net figure; the caption already carries the cash-left and card-growth amounts, and adding a third number on the hero was judged too many. Closed without code change. If revisited, the agreed shape was: net figure in ink, amber chip for cards, red only for bills at risk, caption leading with the safe fact.
  - note (2026-09-09, kevin): 2026-09-09: Kevin saw the mirror case (bills-short hero £803 = £42 cash gap + £761 card growth, red) and chose the cash-led rule instead of the net rule: hero never includes card growth, cards can only floor it at £0. Tracked as G14.

- [x] **G35. Safe-to-Spend moved from £64 to £276 in five hours with zero transactions (Kevin 2026-09-10, both screenshots dated 15 days before payday, confirmed no transactions on the account since 2026-09-09): the £212 rise matches exactly the fall in the coming-up fortnight bill total, £384 down to £172, so the forecast changed while no money moved; seventeen items merged to UAT between the two readings, of which G24 touched services/net_position.py and routers/analytics.py, so establish whether a merged change moved the figure or a scheduled recompute produced a different forecast, then add a regression test that pins safe_to_spend against a fixed transaction fixture so a code change can never silently move a user's headline figure again** [owner: claude] [priority: p1] (done 2026-09-11, 49bda0efdce186c36fa68c1ef1f26feb25010288)
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): Investigation: no merged commit in the 09-09/09-10 window changes the safe_to_spend number itself. G16 (drops card_growth_reserved for cards with a learned repayment series) merged the night before the window; G24 explicitly preserves the reserve math (display-only field). Likely cause: a legitimate scheduled recompute (4h sync cron) reacting to new bank info -- a bill matched to a newly observed PENDING debit is structurally excluded from upcoming_bills, or recurring_judge (capped at 10 judgements/refresh) reaching a series for the first time on a later tick. No point-in-time snapshot survives to confirm which (closed by B18). Sorted stores current as accounts_col.balance (truelayer_sync.py:313-314, finexer_sync.py:603); Safe-to-Spend pools ONLY balance, never available (analytics.py:1175, 1115 _split_balances, via _safe_to_spend_accounts:3818). _match_pending_observed (analytics.py:3227) excludes the matched bill from bills_total once pending, on pending_transactions.py:5-16's premise that current/available both already reflect it -- true only for available. spendable_now (current) stays unchanged while bills_total drops: safe_to_spend rises by the full bill with nothing moved. No live example today: Kevin's only pending row is a GBP0 Klarna hold, matches no bill by amount; B18's safe_to_spend_history_col has 0 docs for every user. Recommend: stop excluding the bill in analytics.py:3213-3230, or prefer accounts_col.available (nets pending) over balance.

  - note (2026-09-11, claude): Coordinator review of the 2026-09-11 investigation: its conclusion (b) rests on TrueLayer's documented formula (available = current + overdraft - pending) plus the fact that Sorted's spendable pool sums accounts_col.balance, which stores the provider 'current' field (truelayer_sync.py:313, analytics.py:1109-1122, projection at :1175 does not even fetch 'available'). But it found NO live example in Kevin's data (his only pending row is a 0.00 Klarna hold) and the B18 snapshot collection is still empty, and it directly contradicts the pending_transactions.py docstring, which says the opposite behaviour was CONFIRMED LIVE on 2026-09-01 via TrueLayer's pending endpoint, the phantom-shortfall bug the exclusion was written to fix. Banks differ: several UK banks move the ledger at authorisation so 'current' already nets pending. Do not act on documentation alone.
  - note (2026-09-11, claude): Decisive test before any change: for one real pending bill per provider on Kevin's accounts, capture accounts_col.balance AND the stored 'available' immediately before and after the pending row appears, and the settled drop afterwards; if balance is unchanged at pending time the exclusion double-counts and should be removed (or the pool should prefer 'available'), if balance already dropped the current behaviour is right and the 2026-09-01 fix stands. The answer may differ per bank, in which case the rule must be per-provider, not global. Safe-to-Spend is fail-closed by doctrine, so if this stays ambiguous the safer default is to keep the bill reserved until it settles.
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Empirical check 2026-09-13 against Kevin's 23 real accounts, read-only: we store BOTH the provider's current and available balances, and six accounts differ. Five are credit cards where available is the remaining credit limit, nothing to do with pending. The sixth is a current account where available is TEN POUNDS HIGHER than current, an arranged buffer, not a pending debit. No account shows available BELOW current, which is the shape a pending debit would produce, and he has exactly one pending row, the zero-pound Klarna hold. So his live data cannot settle this today because there is no real pending debit in flight. What it does show is that available is not simply current minus pending in this data, so the two figures cannot be used interchangeably to infer pending. Likely resolution of the contradiction between the 2026-09-01 live observation and the 2026-09-11 documentation reasoning: a direct debit actually taken reduces CURRENT on the morning it is taken, which is what the original observation saw and what the exclusion was built for, whereas a card authorisation sits in pending and reduces only AVAILABLE. If that is right the rule should distinguish bill-shaped debits from card holds rather than treating all pending alike. Still needs the decisive capture: balance and available immediately before and after a real predicted bill goes pending.

CoverPlanSourcesCard.tsx still takes shortAccountIds as a prop exactly as before (unchanged interface), sourced by the same coverPlanView() in SettingsPage.tsx that derives it from active move-card destinations, so the original bug (an account with zero spare headroom but no live move card shows a normal toggle instead of Skipped) is unchanged. Proposed fix: extract the per-account headroom computation in companion.py (source_capacity/_source_min_running, around line 1780) into a helper callable independent of an active shortfall, and expose it via a new lightweight endpoint, e.g. GET /companion/cover-eligibility returning {account_id: {short: bool, headroom: number}} for every non-credit account, computed the same way the source finder computes it for a live move; SettingsPage would then call this endpoint instead of deriving shortAccountIds from moves.filter(type==="move"). Not attempted in G51: refactoring the live recommendation engine's internals is a real backend change with regression risk, out of scope for a frontend scale/design item, and G45 was concurrently touching SettingsPage.tsx so the change surface there was kept minimal.

- [x] **G71. The cover-plan engine now decides whether a regular move card would be emitted in two separate places that agree only by hand** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 751ddc77d4ef7410152a1770238dd59ecbd2e6c2)
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by the G70 backend review 2026-09-15, flagged rather than fixed because it was outside that item scope. backend/app/services/companion.py section 5d, around lines 2926 to 2960, runs a shadow simulation of whether the regular move card would be emitted, so that it can decide whether to suppress the separate unfunded_move card. The real emission gate lives in section 6, around lines 3204 to 3300. The reviewer traced both line by line and they agree exactly today, same _id formats, same dismissed, existing and cap checks, same iteration order over shortfalls, so nothing is wrong right now. The risk is entirely future: any edit to the real gate that is not mirrored in the shadow copy desyncs them silently, and the failure mode is the one G70 exists to fix, a user told to move money they do not need to move. Extract one shared predicate that both call rather than keeping two copies in step by hand. Note this is the same class as G55 and G65, where eligibility was decided independently on two sides and agreed only by coincidence; the fix there was to make one side authoritative by construction, and the same applies here.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: independently reviewed, SHIP. There is now genuinely ONE predicate, _gate_regular_move_cards, called once before section 5d, with both former duplicate sites only READING its result; a grep for the old duplicated identifiers finds nothing. Sizing and source selection are untouched, confirmed by grepping the diff for amount_needed, _ceil5, credited_incomes and _src_order and finding zero hits, so the engine cannot recommend a different amount or a different source than before. None of the G47, G50, G55, G65, G66 or G70 doctrine is touched. BETTER THAN THE ITEM CLAIMED: the two probes could already disagree TODAY without any future edit, because they were two independent awaited round-trips to Mongo and a concurrent write could make the second answer differently from the first. The one genuinely new test reproduces exactly that with a collection whose second lookup flips its answer, and it fails red against main with the overdue movement vanishing from both cards. So this closes a live race rather than only a maintenance hazard, and the desync class is now structurally impossible rather than merely tested for. One low-severity consequence raised separately: the shared call sits outside the try and except that used to wrap section 5d, so a transient error there now fails the whole brief instead of degrading one card.
- [x] **G72. Period comparison colours a whole sentence orange, and G57 is about to put that in front of every user by making it a default widget** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-14, 0c38d85dec80c3dfbce4689b3ddf4186c120ca75)

It renders a sentence like "£1,307 (36%) more than last" in solid orange text with no separate signifier, which is the case DESIGN.md:142 reserves for a small leading amber dot beside ink text, the same violation G59 fixed on the Settings page cover-plan message. It has been wrong for a while and mattered little while the widget was opt-in. G57 promotes period_compare into the default chart collection, so it stops being a corner a few users opt into and becomes something everyone sees. Fix it the way G59 did, ink text with a small leading amber dot, and while there check whether spending more than last period is even a caution at all: a period that is higher than the last one is not automatically risk, and Red Is Risk plus the calm-cockpit north star both argue against marking an ordinary fluctuation. It may want no signifier rather than a better one.
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-16: reviewed AFTER merge, fine as merged, no action. It answered the harder question rather than only the styling one. Instead of converting the orange sentence to ink plus a small amber dot, it dropped the signifier entirely in BOTH directions, on the stated grounds that an ordinary period-to-period fluctuation is not risk and no upstream signal marks either direction as a real pace threshold. That is the right reading of Red Is Risk and the calm-cockpit north star, and it removes the previous asymmetry where spending more was orange and spending less was emerald, which was the instrument editorialising. Confirmed symmetric in the diff, Money Is Mono preserved on both figures, and the only other change is dropping the percentage, a copy simplification.
- [ ] **G73. Two small drift risks left by the G57 chart-defaults fix: the preview hard-codes the pinned widget, and a dead SpendHeader twin still carries the bug its sibling just fixed** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Raised by the G57 re-review 2026-09-15, both low severity and deliberately not blocking that merge. First: G57 correctly made the previews import DEFAULT_WIDGETS instead of repeating the list, which closed the drift that caused its rejection, but frontend/app/design/spend-live/SpendLiveClient.tsx and frontend/app/design/spend-page-refurbishment/VariantACharts.tsx still hard-code pinnedWidget: "period_compare" as a bare string. There is no frontend-side constant for it, because the default now lives in backend/app/routers/preferences.py as DEFAULT_HOME_PINNED_WIDGET and the frontend is server-driven with PreferencesContext defaulting to null. If the backend default ever changes, both previews go silently stale. Lower stakes than the original defect, since it only mislabels which chart reads as pinned in a mock rather than making a false numeric claim, but it is the same class. Second: frontend/components/SpendHeader.tsx:499, the default-exported SpendHeader, still computes hasMoved as moved_total !== undefined, the very bug G57 fixed in its sibling at :398. Since spend_verdict.py always returns moved_total as a number, zero rather than undefined, that condition is effectively always true, so the component can offer a jump to a Money you moved section that does not render. - [ ] **G77. Penny's cover-plan exclusion write still discards a concurrent Settings change, and the test meant to prove otherwise asserts the loss is correct** [owner: claude] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-14, claude): Found by a post-merge review of G54 (52f81fc) on 2026-09-16. NOT EXPLOITABLE TODAY: Penny agent mode consent is off everywhere per G5, so nothing in production can hit this, which is why it is p2 and not p1. But it must be fixed before that consent is enabled anywhere. G54 shipped TWO different fixes for two write shapes and only one of them works. The Settings side is genuinely right: a single account toggle now sends cover_plan_exclude_add or cover_plan_exclude_remove and the server applies an atomic addToSet or pull with no read before write, so two toggles racing on one device or across two devices both survive, proven by two tests that fail red against the pre-fix code. The Penny side is not. set_cover_plan_exclusions still sends the whole array, now wrapped in a compare-and-set at backend/app/routers/preferences.py:99-160 that reads the version, writes conditioned on it, and retries up to eight times before a 409. On conflict it re-sends the IDENTICAL unmodified array it started with, never re-reading or merging. Traced concretely: Penny reads version 3 with [acc-old] and intends [acc-old, acc-new-penny]; a Settings toggle lands and adds acc-mid-race, version goes to 4; Penny conflicts, loops, re-reads and SEES acc-mid-race, then writes its stale target anyway and discards it. That is a resolved conflict rather than a prevented one, the concurrent writer is silently clobbered exactly as before, just deterministically instead of racily, and it is precisely the clobbering-data-it-never-saw failure G54 was raised to fix. WORSE, THE TEST BLESSES IT: test_cas_full_list_set_retries_past_a_concurrent_delta_and_still_lands at tests/test_cover_plan_exclusions_concurrency.py:253 PASSES against the pre-fix code, because old code does not recognise the delta key the test injects and writes it as an unused literal, so the injected concurrent change never touches the array and the old unconditioned write lands on the asserted state by coincidence. Its own comment at lines 279-281 then calls the discarded write intentional documented semantics. So the lost update was rationalised rather than caught, and the test cannot fail either way. Same shape as A33. FIX: either make the CAS retry genuinely merge, re-deriving the target from the freshly read array rather than resending a stale literal, or re-scope Penny proposal to the same delta shape Settings now uses, which is simpler and makes the two writers agree by construction. Then replace that test with one asserting acc-mid-race is NOT lost, which should fail against both the current and the old code until the merge is real.

- [ ] **G84. The unified cover-plan gate now sits outside the error handling that used to isolate one of its two callers** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Raised by the G71 review 2026-09-16, flagged rather than fixed because G71 is a good change and this is a narrow consequence of it. Before G71, section 5d of backend/app/services/companion.py ran its own shadow probe inside a try and except, so a transient database error there degraded only the unfunded_move card while section 6 own independent probe still ran and could still render move cards. G71 correctly replaced both probes with one shared call, but that call now sits OUTSIDE that try and except, so a transient error in it is uncaught and fails the whole compute_today_items request: no move cards, no unfunded_move card, nothing. The author comment reasons about this and calls it no worse than section 6 existing failure mode, which is true as far as it goes but omits that section 5d failure used to be isolated and no longer is. Narrow, only reachable on a transient database error, and not a money-movement bug. Decide whether the shared gate should catch and degrade, returning an empty gate result so the brief still renders what it can, or whether failing the whole request loudly is actually preferable for a surface that tells someone to move money, since a partially rendered brief built on a failed lookup could be worse than none. Either is defensible; the current state is a side effect rather than a decision.
- [ ] **G85. The spend-containment design index entry lists its three variants as states, so every link it generates shows variant A** [owner: codex] [priority: p2]
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Found by the coordinator 2026-09-16 immediately after G78 merged. frontend/app/design/page.tsx registers the spend-containment preview with states A cockpit card, B unified deck and C editorial ledger, valued a, b and c. But SpendContainmentClient.tsx:282 reads the variant from ?variant= and accepts only b or c, falling back to a; ?state= is read separately at :284 as a VERDICT state from its own list, falling back to normal. So the index generates ?state=a, b or c, the client ignores it, and all three index entries render variant A. The same mistake is in the uat link recorded on G78. Fix by registering the three as variants rather than states, matching how the client actually reads them, or by changing the client to read ?state= if that is the intended contract, but one of the two must move so the index and the client agree. WORTH GENERALISING: this cannot be caught by checking status codes, because the switch happens client-side after hydration, so every combination returns 200 with byte-identical server-rendered HTML; it is only visible by reading the client own parameter parsing or by screenshotting each link and comparing. G57 had the same class of problem when its parameters changed from variant to charts and surface mid-round and the recorded link went stale. Consider whether the design-index check script, which already enforces that every preview directory is indexed, could also assert that each registered parameter name is one the client actually reads.

- [x] **G102. Ten of the eleven Canvas Before Cards previews omit the global navigation, so no round can show a colour or spacing collision with Penny's nav button** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-16, 834b00a030dfe26d290433542129ec8745bb30b4)
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Found by the sweep across eleven rounds 2026-09-16. Only the Accounts round, G87, renders the persistent navigation, using the shared FixtureBottomNav it added under app/design/_components/. The other ten render only their own preview switcher. This is the exact criticism made when G88 was rejected, that omitting the nav hides a possible collision between a page hero and Penny gradient nav button, which uses the same base hue, and it is now repeated across almost the whole family rather than corrected. It matters for this programme specifically: Canvas Before Cards is ABOUT what a page looks like when its boundaries come off, and the persistent nav is the one boundary that never comes off, so judging a canvas layout without it is judging an incomplete screen. The fix is cheap because the shared component already exists: render FixtureBottomNav in each round the way G87 does. Worth doing before Kevin picks from any of them, since a pick made without the nav may not survive contact with it.
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Coordinator 2026-09-17: cleared to merge. This closes the systemic gap raised after the eleven-round sweep, where only the Accounts round rendered the persistent navigation and the other ten showed a canvas layout without the one boundary that never comes off. Eleven files change, adding the shared FixtureBottomNav across the rounds and removing three hand-rolled nav replicas, which matters because a replica cannot catch the real component drifting. Preview-only, nothing under frontend/components touched. It lands alongside G79, which moves the production BottomNav into app/layout.tsx so no page has to remember it, so the previews and the app now agree about where the rail comes from.

- [ ] **H45. Literal pixel font sizes are the standing convention in production code while DESIGN.md defines a named type scale, and every design agent flags it and leaves it (counted 2026-09-14): 703 uses of text-[10px] through text-[13px] across 91 production files outside app/design/, and in this session alone the impeccable hook raised it on CoverPlanSourcesCard, SettingsPage, HomeBrief, PlanPicker and the design index, each time judged pre-existing and out of scope, which is the correct call per item but means nobody ever owns it; DESIGN.md names Body at 13 to 14px, Caption at 12px and Label at 10 to 11px uppercase and tracked, so the literals are not all wrong, the problem is that there is no way to tell a deliberate Label from a drifted Caption; decide first whether the named scale is still what Kevin wants, then either express it as tokens or utility classes so the intent is readable, or write into DESIGN.md that literals are acceptable and delete the rule, since a rule nobody enforces is worse than none; do NOT start by sweeping 703 call sites, start by deciding the rule, and expect this to need Kevin's view on the scale itself** [owner: claude]
- [x] **H46. integrate.py writes raw pytest output into an item's block note, which is the H38 corruption returning by the same route** [owner: claude] [priority: p2] (done 2026-09-15, 0c8cdccf8bfcfd783a53db10939a0be732dab5e7)
  - note (2026-09-13, claude): Found 2026-09-15 while reading G57. When integrate.py blocks an item on a failing backend suite it writes the captured pytest stdout straight into the block reason, so the board grows notes that read "backend test suite failed: / ........ [ 3%] / ........ [ 6%]" for hundreds of characters, carrying no information at all: the dots are progress output, and the actual failing test name and assertion are buried or truncated away. G57 and B29 both carry one right now, and H38 previously had to repair 24 lines of exactly this. The board is what Kevin reads at /ops/go-live, so these notes are pure noise on the page they are meant to inform. Fix at the source in scripts/integrate.py rather than by cleaning up after it: capture the pytest output, extract only the short test summary lines, the FAILED test ids and their assertion text, and write at most a few lines into the note, keeping the full log on disk or in the journal if it is wanted. Then repair the existing corrupted notes. Worth checking whether the same unbounded-capture path is used for frontend build failures and lint failures, which would corrupt the board the same way.
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Second half done 2026-09-16: repaired the two corrupted notes the review found still standing, B33 and G57 (originally item text said B29, that was wrong, both note-level and cross-referenced: B29 carries no such note, B33 and G57 do, each with the identical pre-H46 raw-pytest dump cut off mid test body at "pack = _pack(UID, remaining=250"). Chose APPEND a correction over rewrite-in-place, following the C13/C14 precedent rather than H38: H38's damage was structural, dangling state tags and stray lines that broke the parser, so repair_todo (the H38 lint/apply tool) rewrites those in place by design, and its own docstring says it deliberately never touches a well-formed note. Our two notes parse fine; they are just low-information. backlog.py also has no command to replace an existing note's text at all, only note (append), so rewriting in place would have meant adding a new capability purely to save space, cutting against the board's append-only audit-trail design for no real gain. Recovered the diagnosis the corrupted notes cut off before reaching: both failures are the same test, test_get_subscription_serves_mcp_block_and_packs in backend/tests/test_mcp_call_packs.py, failing on assert result["billing_live"] is False (actual True) because it read the ambient BILLING_ENABLED flag B25's Stripe sandbox bring-up had just flipped true in backend/.env, rather than a pinned value; this is the exact bug B34 already diagnosed and fixed (commit 9a0f444422b6222e04a1d0b81dfb1d12534f6a53, test now pins via patch_billing_enabled). Verified by reading the current test file, not guesswork. Added one clarifying follow-up note on B33 since its first correction said "the note directly above" when a same-dated unrelated note actually sat between them; G57's correction was worded to quote the corrupted note's exact opening text instead, avoiding the ambiguity from the start. Corrupted-note grep count (20+ dot run) unchanged at 2 before and after by design, both left intact as the historical record; no other item in TODO.md carries the shape. backend/.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_backlog.py tests/test_integrate_script.py -q: 190 passed. No code changed, board-only repair via scripts/backlog.py note from the shared tree; worktree is clean and already in sync with origin.

- [ ] **H49. Five frontend check scripts exist and are wired into nothing, so they can silently rot** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Raised by the B39 review 2026-09-16. B39 added frontend/scripts/penny-screen-views.test.mjs, and the reviewer proved it genuine by mutating a label string and watching two checks go red, so it is a real test rather than a shape assertion. But npm run check:penny-screen-views is wired into neither scripts/session.sh finish nor scripts/integrate.py, both of which only run check:design-index and check:legal-content. It is not alone: check:preferences-version, check:serial-queue, check:preference-save and check:preferences-snapshot are all equally unwired, and each of those guards a preference-write invariant that this board has been burned by repeatedly across G45, G52, G58, G60 and G62. So five genuine guards exist and nothing runs them, which means they pass forever regardless of the code and will quietly stop matching reality. This is not a B39 regression, it is a pre-existing pattern B39 happened to add to. Decide whether these belong in session.sh finish, in integrate.py before the merge, or in both, and note the cost: integrate already runs the whole backend suite before merging ANY item, so adding slow checks there blocks the entire board when one fails, which argues for finish rather than integrate. Then add whatever is agreed and confirm each script actually fails the gate when its invariant is broken, rather than trusting that it is wired.
- [ ] **H50. The Numbers Lead Rule only governs figures on cards, and G78 has just made plain canvas the default** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-15, claude): Raised by the G78 review 2026-09-16, a gap rather than a defect, and not a reason to have held that merge. DESIGN.md:167 states the Numbers Lead Rule as "On any card the money figure is the visually heaviest element; its label sits above or beside it in whisper-label style." That scoping was fine when almost everything lived on a card. G78 has now made the page canvas the default surface for orientation and explanation, so a growing share of the app figures will sit on canvas, where no rule states their weight at all. This is not hypothetical: the G57 review already observed that the new Spend journey money figures read LIGHTER than the headline sentence above them, in a container the doctrine explicitly calls plain canvas rather than a card, which sidesteps the rule as written. That observation was recorded and left, and G78 has now generalised the pattern it came from. Decide whether the Numbers Lead Rule should be rescoped from "on any card" to any surface presenting a financial figure, or whether canvas figures deserve a different and deliberately quieter treatment because they are narrative rather than instrument. Either is defensible and it is a Kevin-facing question about what the app sounds like, not a mechanical fix; what is not defensible is the current state, where the rule simply stops applying at the moment the container disappears. Note the PRODUCT.md north star that verdicts lead, which argues the figure should still carry weight wherever it sits.
- [ ] **H51. integrate.py rebuilds the frontend in place in the shared tree, so an interrupted or overlapping build leaves the live UAT site serving a broken build** [owner: claude] [priority: p1]
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Raised 2026-09-17 after a real UAT outage Kevin reported as a blank white page. SEQUENCE OF EVENTS. At 01:33 a build wrote into /root/ai-wealth-dashboard/frontend/.next and did not finish: BUILD_ID was absent and .next/standalone/server.js was absent, while build, server, static and the manifests were present, which is the shape of a build interrupted partway. The already-running next start process kept serving from its in-memory manifest, so the site stayed up but degraded: the cached pre-rendered HTML for / referenced content-hashed chunks that no longer existed on disk, requests for them returned 500 with content type text/plain, the browser refused to execute a script with that MIME type, React never hydrated, and the page rendered genuinely blank with an empty body and no thrown exception. /design rendered but completely unstyled because its CSS chunk 404d despite existing on disk. Restarting the service then killed the only process that could serve anything, and next start refused to boot at all without BUILD_ID, so the site went from degraded to fully down and crash-looped 25 times before it was stopped. Restored by rebuilding in the shared tree and starting the service, verified in a real browser: / now renders the styled sign-in card and /design renders with styling. THE ROOT PROBLEM IS STRUCTURAL, not the interrupted build itself. scripts/integrate.py rebuilds the frontend IN PLACE in the shared tree, which is the same directory the live wealth-frontend service r...
- [ ] **H52. The frontend is built as standalone output but served with next start, which Next warns against on every boot** [owner: claude]
  - note (2026-09-16, claude): Noticed 2026-09-17 while diagnosing the blank-page outage; NOT the cause of it, and deliberately not changed during the incident. frontend/next.config.ts:221 sets output to standalone for any self-hosted build, meaning anything that is not a Vercel build and not the mobile export. The systemd unit at /etc/systemd/system/wealth-frontend.service runs /usr/bin/node node_modules/.bin/next start -p 3030. Next logs a warning on every single boot: next start does not work with output standalone configuration, use node .next/standalone/server.js instead. It has evidently worked anyway for weeks, so this is not urgent, but two things make it worth settling. First, running a configuration the framework explicitly warns against means the failure modes are undocumented, and during this outage a missing chunk returned 500 with content type text/plain rather than a clean 404, which is what made the page blank rather than merely broken; whether that ungraceful behaviour is related is unproven but plausible. Second, it means the build produces .next/standalone/server.js that nothing ever runs, so the intent is ambiguous to the next person reading it. Decide one way or the other: either serve with node .next/standalone/server.js as Next intends, updating the unit and checking the static and public asset copying that standalone requires, or stop building standalone for this host so the config matches how it is actually served. Do not do this during an incident.
